It is a shared reasoning and handoff contract, not a mandatory new document. Scale it to the change: a few sentences for a helper fix, a compact scenario table for a cross-component mutation. Omit empty boilerplate; explain briefly why an operational dimension is irrelevant.\n\n## Minimum useful record\n\n| Field | Content |\n| --- | --- |\n| Intent and authority | Intended outcomes and constraints; source locations; explicit, inferred or unresolved status. |\n| Change boundary | Current task/request, resolved diff scope, candidate revision/worktree state, affected components and deliberately pending functionality. |\n| Existing behavior | Entry points, unchanged callers/consumers, state/data contracts and invariants to preserve. |\n| Delivery constraints | Applicable repository policy, independent/ordered deployment expectations, flags, migrations, prerequisites and accepted exceptions. |\n| Baselines | Review base and candidate; separately, relevant released/deployed revisions, configurations and data shapes; explicit unknowns. |\n| Scenarios and evidence | Success, boundaries, failure/retry and lifecycle cases that apply; source/test bindings, observed results and unverified conditions. |\n\nUser decisions and applicable repository instructions establish requirements. Consult current design/task contracts and relevant linked issues without assuming an issue is newer or more authoritative. Explain consequential conflicts. Existing code establishes behavior, not product intent; tests can repeat an implementation's mistaken assumption. Without a specification, infer a provisional purpose from the request and inspected evidence and label it. Ask only when materially different interpretations prevent a useful judgment; continue independent investigation.\n\nNever equate the review base, local main, a stable tag and a deployed revision. Name the baseline actually inspected. Source compatibility with a tag does not verify a deployed environment. Missing documents or production access narrow conclusions; they neither disable functional reasoning nor create automatic access or approval requirements.\n\n## Producer and reviewer responsibilities\n\nThe producer supplies facts with file/symbol or revision references, separating assumptions, accepted decisions and unavailable evidence. Author explanations and claimed test results remain attributed inputs, not independent verification. Reviewers challenge inferences and request specific missing evidence.\n\nRefresh affected fields after scope/requirement changes or review fixes. On resume, inspect current repository state rather than trusting a stale handoff. In plan mode, record dated observations, sources, proposals, accepted decisions and unresolved actions in the current task's labeled **Execution context** subsection in `tasks.md`. Observations do not amend specifications; requirement changes need explicit user authorization with source and rationale. Standalone context stays conversational unless material work is deferred: use existing tracking or a concise repository-local review note naming owner (or unassigned), reason, next action and verification condition.\n\n## Historical evidence\n\nWhen compatibility depends on source absent from candidate files or diff hunks, the parent workflow obtains the relevant local Git blob or bounded excerpt. For independent reviewers, materialize actual source in a read-only evidence bundle outside the reviewed worktree. Label repository identity, full revision, original path, blob/content hash, original line span and omitted/redacted extent. Distinguish historical source from candidate files and methodology; give both reviewers the same relevant evidence, not merely the author's summary.\n\nA reviewer requests further evidence by naming repository, revision, path/symbol and the comparison needed. The parent supplies available local source through resume/reinvoke with original context and provisional findings, and supplies equivalent relevant additions through PAL continuation. Retain request provenance. This does not require reviewer shell permissions or authorize otherwise unapproved network/production access. Unavailable source or an inconclusive bounded investigation leaves the specific conclusion **Unknown**, with the missing evidence and next action.\n## Profile detection procedure\n\nSingle source of truth for computing the set of profiles active in the current context.\nConsumed by six skills: `$kk:review-code`, `$kk:review-spec`, `$kk:design`, `$kk:implement`, `$kk:test`, and `$kk:document`.\n\nEvery profile under `klaude-plugin/profiles/<name>/` declares its own trigger rule in `DETECTION.md` using the mandatory three-section schema (`## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, `## Content signals`).\nThe shared procedure below applies the same algorithm against every profile's declared values.\n\n### Inputs per consuming skill\n\nNot every consumer has a diff available. Use the input listed for your skill:\n\n- **`$kk:review-code`** \u2014 git diff (staged, or an explicit commit range). Detection uses idea-prose keyword matching against tokens declared in each profile's `DETECTION.md`.\n\n**Algorithm:**\n\n1. **Collect tokens.** Iterate \u00a7Known profiles. For each `<name>`, `Read` `../../profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`. If the file has no `## Design signals` section, skip \u2014 that profile does not participate in design-phase detection. Otherwise, parse `display_name` and `tokens` from the section.\n2. **Build union.** Collect all declared tokens into a single set, each tagged by its source profile name and `display_name`.\n3. **Match.** Check the idea prose against the union. Matching is case-insensitive, whole-word (so `pod` in \"podcast\" does not fire).\n4. **Confirm.** On match, surface a confirmation prompt per matched profile:\n   *\"This appears to be a {display_name} feature. Activate the {profile_name} profile?\"* \u2014 let the user confirm yes/no. When multiple profiles match, confirm each independently.\n5. **Fallback.** If no token matches but the idea is **ambiguous** \u2014 names infrastructure, deployment, runtime, or platform concerns without naming a specific technology (e.g., _\"add a caching layer for the service\"_, _\"build a CI pipeline\"_, _\"deploy to production\"_); or includes overloaded tokens that collide across domains \u2014 build the fallback prompt dynamically from all profiles that declare `## Design signals`:\n   *\"Does this feature involve {display_name_1, display_name_2, ...}? If yes, which?\"*\n\nConfirmation is required \u2014 the $kk:design skill never auto-activates a profile silently. The narrow per-profile token sets avoid noisy false positives from tokens that overload across domains.\n\nOnce activated, subsequent design-phase steps treat the profile as active in the same record shape produced by file-based detection (see \u00a7Output shape).\n\n### Known profiles\n\nThis is the authoritative enumeration of profile `<name>`s \u2014 do NOT try discover profiles via any other means.\nAn explicit list is boring, deterministic, and unambiguous; runtime filesystem enumeration against the plugin tree has proven unreliable.\n\n- `go`\n- `python`\n- `java`\n- `js_ts`\n- `kotlin`\n- `k8s`\n- `k8s-operator`\n- `skill-md`\n\n### Algorithm\n\nThis procedure reads files under the plugin root. The main agent resolves the plugin root from the installed skill's absolute `SKILL.md` path (the plugin root is the parent of the `skills/` directory) and passes that absolute path to sub-agents under `## Plugin Root`; a Read-only sub-agent uses the absolute plugin-root path injected into its prompt under `## Plugin Root` (see its agent definition). Substitute that resolved path for the plugin-root prefix in every `\u2026/profiles/\u2026` read below.\n\n1. **Iterate profiles.** For each \u00a7Known profiles `<name>`:\n   1. Use the `Read` tool on `../../profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`.\n   2. If `Read` fails with ENOENT (profile name in list but directory missing \u2014 a stale list entry), skip silently and move on.\n   3. If `Read` succeeds, parse the declared `## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, and `## Content signals` sections.\n\n2. **Evaluate in cost order.** For each input file, check signals in this order: path \u2192 filename \u2192 content. Cheapest first.\n3. **Apply the authority rule.** A file activates the profile only if a **filename signal** OR **content signal** matches.\n   A path-only match does NOT activate. Paths are a pre-filter that promotes files to \"candidates\"; authoritative activation requires filename or content confirmation.\n   A file that matches NO path signal is still evaluated against filename and content signals \u2014 path pre-filtering is a cost hint, not a gate.\n   (Otherwise a `Chart.yaml` at a non-standard path would be missed.)\n4. **Bound content inspection.** Read at most ~16 KB per file when evaluating content signals.\n   Multi-document YAML is inspected per `---`-separated block \u2014 a file may have five blocks, and only the third need match for the file to activate the profile.\n5. **Collect records.** Accumulate one record per matched profile with the triggering files and the signal descriptions that fired.\n\n### Tool choice\n\nA consumer may consolidate instruction reads into bounded packets containing\nthe complete, unmodified source bodies, original paths and hashes. Read every\npacket part completely before applying those instructions; the original paths\nstill determine link resolution and detection provenance. A manifest, checksum,\nrequested read or truncated packet alone does not establish loading. This\nchanges packaging only: evaluate every Known-profile rule and each index\nconditional exactly as below. Direct file reads remain the default fallback.\n\n- Single file at `../../\u2026` \u2192 `Read`. This is what the algorithm uses.\n- Enumeration across profiles \u2192 iterate the \u00a7Known profiles list, `Read` each. Never `Glob` (cwd-scoped, misses outside-cwd paths).\n\n### Two dimensions: cost vs authority\n\nSignals live on two axes that point in different directions. Keep them separate in your mental model:\n\n- **Evaluation cost** (cheapest first): path < filename < content.\n  Path globs touch only the path string;\n  filename matches are exact string compares;\n  content inspection opens the file.\n- **Authority** (most authoritative first): filename \u2248 content > path.\n  A filename or content match activates the profile; a path-only match does not.\n  Filename and content are equally authoritative, but filename resolves first at runtime \u2014 a filename match short-circuits content inspection for that file.\n\nEvaluating cheapest-first optimizes work. Applying authority correctly prevents false positives from incidental path matches \u2014 a stray `manifests/` directory in a Go project does not make the project Kubernetes.\n\n### Plugin-root resolution failure\n\nIf every `Read` attempt in Algorithm step 1 fails \u2014 i.e., the plugin root could not be resolved (the installed skill path is unavailable to the main agent, or no `## Plugin Root` path was provided to a sub-agent) or the paths do not exist \u2014 the procedure cannot continue.\n\nOn that failure:\n\n1. Emit an actionable error pointing to `CLAUDE.md` \u00a7Profile Conventions.\n2. Return an empty result set so the calling skill falls back to generic guidance rather than panicking.\n3. Do not retry; do not silently guess a path.\n\nConsumers inherit this check by invoking the shared procedure \u2014 no skill re-implements it.\n\n### Output shape\n\nA list of records, one per matched profile:\n\n```\n[\n  {\n    profile: \"<name>\",                     // directory name under profiles/\n    triggered_by: [\n      \"filename: Chart.yaml\",              // signal type + matched value\n      \"content: apiVersion+kind in block 2\"\n    ],\n    files: [\n      \"path/to/file1.yaml\",\n      \"path/to/file2.yaml\"\n    ]\n  },\n  ...\n]\n```\n\nField semantics:\n\n- `profile` \u2014 the directory name under `profiles/` (e.g., `go`, `python`, `k8s`).\n  Used downstream to resolve `profiles/<profile>/<phase>/index.md`,\n  where `<phase>` is the profile phase subdirectory named identically to the calling skill:\n  `review-code/`, `review-spec/`, `design/`, `implement/`, `test/`, or `document/`.\n- `triggered_by` \u2014 which signal type fired and the specific value that matched.\n  For debugging and for explaining detection to the user; never used as the key for profile lookup.\n- `files` \u2014 the subset of input files that activated this profile.\n  Skills use this to scope behavior (e.g., `helm lint` runs only on files triggered under Helm filename signals, not on every YAML in the diff).\n\nWhen no profile matches, return the empty list `[]`. The caller falls back to generic guidance, identical to today's \"no language detected\" path.\n# Standalone Mode\n\nApplies for bug fixes, GitHub issues, one-off tasks, and any work without docs/feat/wip infrastructure.\n\n## Entry Procedure\n\nAfter the basic instructions in SKILL.md load, collect request/target metadata only.     "aggregatedOutput": "# Kubernetes \u2014 detection\n\nDeclares when the `k8s` profile activates on a given set of files. Consumed by `klaude-plugin/skills/_shared/profile-detection.md`. Detection is additive: multiple profiles may activate on the same diff (e.g., `go` + `k8s`).\n\nEvaluation follows the shared cost-ordered procedure (path \u2192 filename \u2192 content). Authority runs filename \u2248 content > path: filename or content signals activate the profile; path alone never does, it only promotes a file to a candidate.\n\n## Path signals\n\nCase-insensitive substring match anywhere in the file's path. Pre-filter only \u2014 a path hit alone does NOT activate the profile.\n\n- `k8s/`\n- `manifests/`\n- `charts/`\n- `kustomize/`\n- `deploy/`\n- `templates/`\n\n## Filename signals\n\nAuthoritative: any match activates the profile. Filename matches short-circuit content inspection for the matched file.\n\n- `Chart.yaml` (exact) \u2192 Helm chart root.\n- Any filename starting with `values` (e.g., `values.yaml`, `values.yml`, `values-prod.yaml`, `values-prod-v2-final.yaml`) when the containing directory also contains `Chart.yaml` \u2192 Helm values by adjacency. The `values*` glob has no upper bound on the wildcard; the adjacency rule (sibling `Chart.yaml` in the same directory) is the binding constraint. The match is filename-plus-adjacency only \u2014 file content is not inspected, so a file named `values-backup.yaml` next to a `Chart.yaml` activates regardless of what it actually contains.\n- Any file with extension `.yaml`, `.yml`, or `.tpl` inside `<dir>/templates/` where `<dir>` itself contains a `Chart.yaml` as a direct child \u2192 Helm template. The binding constraint is that the `templates/` directory must sit *directly* next to a `Chart.yaml` \u2014 i.e., at a chart root or a subchart root under `<parent>/charts/<subchart>/`. A `templates/` nested elsewhere in the tree (e.g., `docs/templates/`, `ci/templates/`) does NOT activate this rule even when a `Chart.yaml` sits at the repository root, because `docs/` and `ci/` do not themselves contain a `Chart.yaml`. This avoids the monorepo false-positive where a repo-root umbrella `Chart.yaml` would otherwise claim every `templates/` directory in the tree. It still avoids the trap where a standalone edit to `<chart-root>/templates/deployment.yaml` contains `{{ if ... }}` directives before any `apiVersion:` and would otherwise fail the content signal.\n- Exact filenames `kustomization.yaml`, `kustomization.yml`, or `Kustomization` \u2192 Kustomize.\n\n## Content signals\n\nAuthoritative for generic YAML files (`.yaml` or `.yml`) not already caught by a filename signal. Inspection is bounded to the first ~16 KB per file; large generated manifests beyond that bound are not inspected.\n\n- Split the file on `---` document separators. For each `---`-separated document block, check for a top-level `apiVersion:` AND a top-level `kind:` \u2014 parsed as YAML mapping keys at zero indent, not as substrings inside block scalars (`|`, `>`) or comments. A block satisfying both is a Kubernetes manifest document.\n- One matching document activates the profile for that file. The first document need not match \u2014 a file whose second or later document is the only K8s document still activates.\n- A `.yaml` / `.yml` file with no matching document in any block \u2192 not Kubernetes. (It may still match another profile; generic YAML belongs to no profile by default.)\n\n---\n\n## Multi-profile behavior\n\nThe Kubernetes profile is **additive**. It coexists with programming-language profiles or any other IaC profile on the same diff. When Go source files sit alongside Kubernetes manifests, both `go` and `k8s` activate; downstream skills consult both profiles' content and emit findings grouped by `(profile, checklist)`.\n\n## Design signals\n\ndisplay_name: Kubernetes\ntokens:\n  - Kubernetes\n  - K8s\n  - Helm chart\n  - kubectl\n  - kustomize\n  - manifest.yaml\n  - Deployment resource\n  - StatefulSet\n  - DaemonSet\n  - CronJob\n\n## Dockerfile non-trigger\n\nA Dockerfile on its own \u2014 even under a `deploy/` or `k8s/` directory \u2014 does NOT activate the `k8s` profile. Dockerfiles match no filename signal here (they are not `Chart.yaml` / `values*.yaml` / `kustomization.yaml`) and no content signal (they do not contain `apiVersion:` + `kind:`). When a Dockerfile appears in the same diff as Kubernetes manifests, `k8s` activates on the manifests' signals; the Dockerfile itself is not reviewed by this profile. A future container profile may own Dockerfiles independently.\n",

Do not reproduce the implementation's calculation as the expected result or assert private call sequences without a behavioral reason.\n- For a bug fix, add the smallest regression that fails for the original defect and passes with the correction. Verify both outcomes when practical. Respect a verification-only request: report gaps without changing source or tests.\n- Choose boundary cases that can alter behavior: absent versus falsey values, empty inputs, invalid data, repeated calls with mutable defaults, iterator exhaustion, and partial failure. Use named parametrized cases or subtests where they clarify the contract.\n- Assert the specific expected exception and relevant context. An unrelated exception must not satisfy a test intended to prove input validation or cleanup.\n\n## Keep state and resources isolated\n\n- Each test should be independently runnable. Restore patched environment variables, working directories, globals, caches, and clocks; do not depend on execution order.\n- Prefer temporary paths and controlled collaborators to real user files, live services, wall-clock timing, or nondeterministic random values. Exercise real integration boundaries through the project's established fixtures when that is the behavior under test.\n- Give mutable fixtures the narrowest useful lifetime. Shared session/module fixtures must not leak state between tests.\n- Register cleanup as acquisition succeeds. A pytest yield fixture that fails before reaching `yield` cannot run the teardown after that yield; use smaller fixtures or appropriate cleanup registration for partial setup. See [pytest fixture teardown](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/fixtures.html#safe-teardowns).\n- Verify resources are released on failures as well as on success. Assertions should distinguish an empty successful result from an error that was swallowed.\n\n## Use mocks at boundaries\n\n- Patch the name where the code under test looks it up. Prefer a small fake or a mock constrained to the collaborator's interface; use autospeccing where it helps expose invalid calls.\n- Keep the behavior under test real. Mock network/time/process boundaries rather than replacing the function whose result the test claims to verify.\n- Match synchronous and asynchronous protocols. Calling an `AsyncMock` and awaiting it are separate events; use awaited-call assertions when the contract requires awaiting. See [unittest.mock](https://docs.python.org/3/library/unittest.mock.html).\n\n## Match verification to the requirement\n\n- Use existing property-based tools for meaningful invariants and existing benchmark tooling for performance claims. Do not add a test framework, benchmark dependency, or a fragile wall-clock threshold to every change.\n- Preserve the project's coverage configuration and thresholds. Coverage indicates execution, not correctness; useful assertions and failure paths matter more than a percentage alone.\n- For stubs and annotation changes, verify public typing contracts with the configured checker and, when available, relevant runtime tests. Syntax parsing alone does not establish stub/runtime consistency.\n# Python test execution and validators\n\n## Select the project and environment first\n\n1. Identify the package or subproject owning the changed Python files. Read its test instructions, CI commands, task scripts, and relevant configuration before choosing a runner. In a monorepo, a neighboring package's tooling is not evidence for this one.\n2. Prefer the user-specified or established project entry point, including existing tox/nox sessions, package-manager wrappers, or framework test commands. Preserve its working directory, dependency groups, discovery patterns, markers, and plugin options.\n3. Check supported Python versions and the intended environment. Use that environment's interpreter and tools; a global executable may run different dependencies. Do not raise the project's runtime floor or replace its runner to accommodate the local machine.\n4. Before invoking a command, check its executable (`command -v` for a PATH command, an executable-file check for an explicit path). For interpreter modules, check availability in that same interpreter, such as `importlib.util.find_spec(\"pytest\")`. Wrapper-managed environments must be checked through their established environment mechanism, not the global Python installation.\n5. If a required environment or tool is missing, follow the project's declared setup when environment setup is within task scope. Otherwise report the check as unavailable with the missing prerequisite and the project's setup instructions, and continue independent checks. Do not blindly execute a missing path, install a global substitute, or change dependencies merely to produce a passing result.\n\n## Run applicable checks\n\nUse existing commands and settings first. The examples below use `python` to mean the selected project interpreter, with its usual wrapper when needed.\n\n| Project evidence | Check to run |\n| --- | --- |\n| A documented test command or CI test session | Run that entry point with its configured environment and selectors. A direct runner invocation is only an equivalent substitute when its setup and options are preserved. |\n| Pytest tests/configuration, without another required entry point | Use `python -m pytest` from the configured root. Preserve discovery settings such as `testpaths` and `python_files`; passing an explicit directory can change which tests are collected. |\n| A unittest suite, without another required entry point | Use `python -m unittest` with the project's module names or `discover` arguments. Preserve the start directory, filename pattern, and import root; for example `discover -s tests -t .` requires that layout to be importable. |\n| Configured formatter, linter, type checker, or coverage gate | Run its existing check command in the selected environment. Keep versions, options, and thresholds; use check mode rather than automatic rewriting during verification. A tool merely installed globally is not a project requirement. |\n| `.pyi` changes | Run the existing type/stub checks and relevant runtime suite where available. Do not execute a stub as a program or claim that compilation proves its types match an implementation. |\n\nDo not replace missing pytest with unittest discovery: pytest functions and fixtures are not a unittest suite. See [pytest invocation](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/usage.html) and [unittest discovery](https://docs.python.org/3/library/unittest.html#test-discovery).\n\nStart with focused tests when useful for diagnosis, then run the required project suite and configured checks. Once they pass, repeat only for new edits, failures, or unresolved concerns. For integration suites, use the established local services/fixtures; if unavailable, state the missing coverage. Report supported-version matrix entries that could not be exercised instead of claiming the local interpreter validates every version.\n\n## Inspect outcomes, not just exit codes\n\n- Record the command, working directory, interpreter/environment, exit status, and executed/failed/skipped test counts when the runner provides them.\n- Zero executed tests is not runtime verification, even with exit status zero. Inspect discovery, selectors, and skip reasons. Pytest normally returns exit code 5 for no collected tests. Unittest's no-test exit behavior varies by Python version: older versions can report `Ran 0 tests` followed by `OK`, so always inspect the count. Collection-only, import, and syntax checks are separate checks, not a passing runtime suite. See [pytest exit codes](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/reference/exit-codes.html).\n- Treat import errors, plugin/configuration errors, unawaited-coroutine warnings, and unexpectedly skipped async tests as verification gaps or failures. Do not hide them by weakening assertions, changing markers to exclude failures, suppressing relevant warnings, or lowering configured thresholds.\n- Report each applicable check as passed, failed, or unavailable with evidence. If a required check could not run, state that verification is incomplete. Omit inventories of optional tools the project has not adopted.\n",

Genuinely unreachable unfinished paths need not be implemented merely because they are planned.\n```\n\nWhen shape 3 applies, use:\n\n```\n## Task Scope\n\nNo task scope available \u2014 review the diff on its own merits without assuming any feature-level design doc.\n```\n\n### Determining mode\n\n- **mid-implementation** \u2014 at least one task in `tasks.md` has status `pending` or `in-progress`.\n- **post-implementation** \u2014 all tasks are `done`.\n\nIn shape 1, the invoking workflow (`$kk:implement`) may pass the current task id directly; that task is automatically in scope even if `tasks.md` hasn't been updated yet.\n\n### Interpretation guidance for reviewers\n\nThe reviewer prompt must also carry this instruction (agent definitions should state it; the workflow relays it implicitly via the scope block):\n\n- Do **not** report missing-implementation findings for items covered only by out-of-scope tasks.\n- Do still report real issues *within* the in-scope tasks, including ones that affect future pending work (e.g., a public API the pending task will depend on is shaped wrongly).\n- A current consumer that already requires a pending provider change remains reviewable, including when the larger feature is disabled. A `templates/` nested elsewhere in the tree (e.g., `docs/templates/`, `ci/templates/`) does NOT activate this rule even when a `Chart.yaml` sits at the repository root, because `docs/` and `ci/` do not themselves contain a `Chart.yaml`. links relative to this original source path.\n\n## Profile detection procedure\n\nSingle source of truth for computing the set of profiles active in the current context.\nConsumed by six skills: `$kk:review-code`, `$kk:review-spec`, `$kk:design`, `$kk:implement`, `$kk:test`, and `$kk:document`.\n\nEvery profile under `klaude-plugin/profiles/<name>/` declares its own trigger rule in `DETECTION.md` using the mandatory three-section schema (`## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, `## Content signals`).\nThe shared procedure below applies the same algorithm against every profile's declared values.\n\n### Inputs per consuming skill\n\nNot every consumer has a diff available. Use the input listed for your skill:\n\n- **`$kk:review-code`** \u2014 git diff (staged, or an explicit commit range). Scope is\n  the set of files the diff touches.\n- **`$kk:review-spec`** \u2014 git diff when invoked standalone; the feature directory's\n  full file list when invoked by `$kk:implement` (spec review runs over the whole\n  feature, not just the current task's diff).\n- **`$kk:test`** \u2014 git diff mid-feature, OR the feature directory's file list\n  post-implementation.\n- **`$kk:implement`** \u2014 the current sub-task's target file list, augmented by the\n  diff accumulated so far in the feature.\n- **`$kk:design`** \u2014 **no file list available** (implementation does not yet exist).\n  Detection uses a user-declared or keyword-inferred signal instead; see\n  [The `$kk:design` interaction pattern](#the-design-interaction-pattern) below.\n- **`$kk:document`** \u2014 feature directory's current file list; diff optional.\n\n### The `$kk:design` interaction pattern\n\nThe design phase runs before any code exists, so file-based detection is impossible. Detection uses idea-prose keyword matching against tokens declared in each profile's `DETECTION.md`.\n\n**Algorithm:**\n\n1. **Collect tokens.** Iterate \u00a7Known profiles. For each `<name>`, `Read` `../../profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`. If the file has no `## Design signals` section, skip \u2014 that profile does not participate in design-phase detection. Otherwise, parse `display_name` and `tokens` from the section.\n2. **Build union.** Collect all declared tokens into a single set, each tagged by its source profile name and `display_name`.\n3. **Match.** Check the idea prose against the union. Matching is case-insensitive, whole-word (so `pod` in \"podcast\" does not fire).\n4. **Confirm.** On match, surface a confirmation prompt per matched profile:\n   *\"This appears to be a {display_name} feature. Activate the {profile_name} profile?\"* \u2014 let the user confirm yes/no. When multiple profiles match, confirm each independently.\n5. **Fallback.** If no token matches but the idea is **ambiguous** \u2014 names infrastructure, deployment, runtime, or platform concerns without naming a specific technology (e.g., _\"add a caching layer for the service\"_, _\"build a CI pipeline\"_, _\"deploy to production\"_); or includes overloaded tokens that collide across domains \u2014 build the fallback prompt dynamically from all profiles that declare `## Design signals`:\n\nEND PACKET PART 2/6\nInstruction packet bootstrap: part 3/6\n\n   *\"Does this feature involve {display_name_1, display_name_2, ...}? If yes, which?\"*\n\nConfirmation is required \u2014 the $kk:design skill never auto-activates a profile silently. The narrow per-profile token sets avoid noisy false positives from tokens that overload across domains.\n\nOnce activated, subsequent design-phase steps treat the profile as active in the same record shape produced by file-based detection (see \u00a7Output shape).\n\n### Known profiles\n\nThis is the authoritative enumeration of profile `<name>`s \u2014 do NOT try discover profiles via any other means.\nAn explicit list is boring, deterministic, and unambiguous; runtime filesystem enumeration against the plugin tree has proven unreliable.\n\n- `go`\n- `python`\n- `java`\n- `js_ts`\n- `kotlin`\n- `k8s`\n- `k8s-operator`\n- `skill-md`\n\n### Algorithm\n\nThis procedure reads files under the plugin root. The main agent resolves the plugin root from the installed skill's absolute `SKILL.md` path (the plugin root is the parent of the `skills/` directory) and passes that absolute path to sub-agents under `## Plugin Root`; a Read-only sub-agent uses the absolute plugin-root path injected into its prompt under `## Plugin Root` (see its agent definition). Substitute that resolved path for the plugin-root prefix in every `\u2026/profiles/\u2026` read below.\n\n1. **Iterate profiles.** For each \u00a7Known profiles `<name>`:\n   1. Use the `Read` tool on `../../profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`.\n   2. If `Read` fails with ENOENT (profile name in list but directory missing \u2014 a stale list entry), skip silently and move on.\n   3. If `Read` succeeds, parse the declared `## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, and `## Content signals` sections.\n\n2. **Evaluate in cost order.** For each input file, check signals in this order: path \u2192 filename \u2192 content. Cheapest first.\n3. **Apply the authority rule.** A file activates the profile only if a **filename signal** OR **content signal** matches.\n   A path-only match does NOT activate. Paths are a pre-filter that promotes files to \"candidates\"; authoritative activation requires filename or content confirmation.\n   A file that matches NO path signal is still evaluated against filename and content signals \u2014 path pre-filtering is a cost hint, not a gate.\n   (Otherwise a `Chart.yaml` at a non-standard path would be missed.)\n4. **Bound content inspection.** Read at most ~16 KB per file when evaluating content signals.\n   Multi-document YAML is inspected per `---`-separated block \u2014 a file may have five blocks, and only the third need match for the file to activate the profile.\n5. **Collect records.** Accumulate one record per matched profile with the triggering files and the signal descriptions that fired.\n\n### Tool choice\n\nA consumer may consolidate instruction reads into bounded packets containing\nthe complete, unmodified source bodies, original paths and hashes. Read every\npacket part completely before applying those instructions; the original paths\nstill determine link resolution and detection provenance. A manifest, checksum,\nrequested read or truncated packet alone does not establish loading. This\nchanges packaging only: evaluate every Known-profile rule and each index\nconditional exactly as below. Direct file reads remain the default fallback.\n\n- Single file at `../../\u2026` \u2192 `Read`. This is what the algorithm uses.\n- Enumeration across profiles \u2192 iterate the \u00a7Known profiles list, `Read` each. Never `Glob` (cwd-scoped, misses outside-cwd paths).\n\n### Two dimensions: cost vs authority\n\nSignals live on two axes that point in different directions. Keep them separate in your mental model:\n\n- **Evaluation cost** (cheapest first): path < filename < content.\n  Path globs touch only the path string;\n  filename matches are exact string compares;\n  content inspection opens the file.\n- **Authority** (most authoritative first): filename \u2248 content > path.\n  A filename or content match activates the profile; a path-only match does not.\n  Filename and content are equally authoritative, but filename resolves first at runtime \u2014 a filename match short-circuits content inspection for that file.\n\nEvaluating cheapest-first optimizes work. Applying authority correctly prevents false positives from incidental path matches \u2014 a stray `manifests/` directory in a Go project does not make the project Kubernetes.\n\n### Plugin-root resolution failure\n\nIf every `Read` attempt in Algorithm step 1 fails \u2014 i.e., the plugin root could not be resolved (the installed skill path is unavailable to the main agent, or no `## Plugin Root` path was provided to a sub-agent) or the paths do not exist \u2014 the procedure cannot continue.\n\nOn that failure:\n\n1. Emit an actionable error pointing to `CLAUDE.md` \u00a7Profile Conventions.\n2. Return an empty result set so the calling skill falls back to generic guidance rather than panicking.\n3. Do not retry; do not silently guess a path.\n\nConsumers inherit this check by invoking the shared procedure \u2014 no skill re-implements it.\n\n### Output shape\n\nA list of records, one per matched profile:\n\n```\n[\n  {\n    profile: \"<name>\",                     // directory name under profiles/\n    triggered_by: [\n      \"filename: Chart.yaml\",              // signal type + matched value\n      \"content: apiVersion+kind in block 2\"\n    ],\n    files: [\n      \"path/to/file1.yaml\",\n      \"path/to/file2.yaml\"\n    ]\n  },\n  ...\n]\n```\n\nField semantics:\n\n- `profile` \u2014 the directory name under `profiles/` (e.g., `go`, `python`, `k8s`).\n  Used downstream to resolve `profiles/<profile>/<phase>/index.md`,\n  where `<phase>` is the profile phase subdirectory named identically to the calling skill:\n  `review-code/`, `review-spec/`, `design/`, `implement/`, `test/`, or `document/`.\n- `triggered_by` \u2014 which signal type fired and the specific value that matched.\n  For debugging and for explaining detection to the user; never used as the key for profile lookup.\n- `files` \u2014 the subset of input files that activated this profile.\n  Skills use this to scope behavior (e.g., `helm lint` runs only on files triggered under Helm filename signals, not on every YAML in the diff).\n\nWhen no profile matches, return the empty list `[]`. The caller falls back to generic guidance, identical to today's \"no language detected\" path.\n\nEND SOURCE\n\nBEGIN SOURCE /home/sergio/.codex/plugins/cache/fr-opus55-581dd0e1-20261011/kk/0.23.0/skills/review-code/shared-review-scope-protocol.md\nSHA256 5ce18227e5c411334173dc67da40a7ea728464420b9c85aa26ffa94cca8536a0\nResolve links relative to this original source path.\n\n## Review scope protocol\n\nReviewers need task status alongside the diff and design docs. Without scope, they flag code-that-isn't-written-yet as `MISSING_IMPL` (spec review) or as missing functionality (code review). Standard review uses this scope locally; isolated review injects the same artifact into sub-agent and external-reviewer prompts. The existing $kk:review-spec payload remains sufficient; no additional change-context record is required by this protocol.\n\n**Code-review timing:** apply this protocol's task-document reads during the\npost-instruction investigation phase, after every resolved checklist is loaded.\nEarly diff selection uses filenames and Git metadata; it is distinct from\ndetermining task scope. Reading this protocol does not authorize early inspection\nof a discovered task file's requirements. The calling workflow still governs\nany bounded inspection needed solely for a declared routing predicate.\n\n### Input shapes\n\nThe review workflow must handle three invocation shapes:\n\n1. **Invoked from `$kk:implement`** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists with `design.md`, `implementation.md`, `tasks.md`. The current task id is known (the one just finished). Use full scope filtering.\n2. **Invoked directly inside a feature** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists but no current task is explicitly supplied. Derive scope from `tasks.md` status fields (`done` = in scope, `pending`/`in-progress` = out of scope).\n3. **Invoked on an arbitrary diff** \u2014 no `/docs/feat/wip/` directory relates to the diff. Skip the scope artifact entirely and note \"No task scope available\" in the reviewer prompt.\n\n### Scope artifact format\n\nWhen shape 1 or 2 applies, build this block and inject it into every reviewer prompt (sub-agent prompt template and the external-reviewer `step` framing):\n\n```\n## Task Scope\n\nReview mode: {mid-implementation | post-implementation}\n\nIn scope (review these):\n- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: done\n- [...more done tasks if applicable]\n\nOut of scope (pending \u2014 DO NOT flag as missing):\n- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: {pending|in-progress}\n- [...more pending tasks]\n\nThe feature's design docs describe the full end state. Pending tasks are expected gaps in the current diff \u2014 treat their absence from the code as intentional, not as a finding. You may still flag issues within the in-scope tasks even if they reference pending tasks (e.g., a broken interface contract). Pending work does not excuse current regressions or incompatibility: assess whether the reviewed increment preserves existing flows and meets applicable delivery requirements. Genuinely unreachable unfinished paths need not be implemented merely because they are planned.\n```\n\nWhen shape 3 applies, use:\n\n```\n## Task Scope\n\nNo task scope available \u2014 review the diff on its own merits without assuming any feature-level design doc.\n```\n\n### Determining mode\n\n- **mid-implementation** \u2014 at least one task in `tasks.md` has status `pending` or `in-progress`.\n- **post-implementation** \u2014 all tasks are `done`.\n\nIn shape 1, the invoking workflow (`$kk:implement`) may pass the current task id directly; that task is automatically in scope even if `tasks.md` hasn't been updated yet.\n\n### Interpretation guidance for reviewers\n\nThe reviewer prompt must also carry this instruction (agent definitions should state it; the workflow relays it implicitly via the scope block):\n\n- Do **not** report missing-implementation findings for items covered only by out-of-scope tasks.\n- Do still report real issues *within* the in-scope tasks, including ones that affect future pending work (e.g., a public API the pending task will depend on is shaped wrongly).\n- A current consumer that already requires a pending provider change remains reviewable, including when the larger feature is disabled. Distinguish deployment from activation and check the actual affected paths; a flag or a future task alone proves neither reachability nor safety.\n- Attribute introduced/worsened behavior separately from inherited defects. control (IDOR - Insecure Direct Object Reference)\n- Session fixation or weak session management\n\n## JWT & Token Security\n\n- Algorithm confusion attacks (accepting `none` or `HS256` when expecting (audience)\n\n## Secrets and PII\n\n- API keys, tokens, or credentials in code/config/logs\n- Secrets in git history or environment variables exposed to client\n- Excessive logging of PII or sensitive payloads\n- Missing data masking in error messages\n\n## Supply Chain & Dependencies\n\n- Unpinned dependencies allowing malicious updates (missing version pins in `requirements.txt`/`pyproject.toml`)\n- Dependency confusion (private package name collision on PyPI)\n- Importing from untrusted sources without integrity checks\n- Outdated dependencies with known CVEs\n\n## CORS & Headers\n\n- Overly permissive CORS (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` with credentials)\n- Missing security headers (CSP, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options)\n- Exposed internal headers or stack traces\n\n## Runtime Risks\n\n- Unbounded loops, recursive calls, or large in-memory buffers\n- Missing timeouts, retries, or rate limiting on external calls\n- Blocking operations on the event loop (sync I/O in `async` context)\n- Resource exhaustion (file handles, connections, memory)\n- **`assert` stripped in optimized mode**: `python -O` removes all `assert` statements \u2014 never use `assert` for security/input validation\n- ReDoS (Regular Expression Denial of Service)\n\n## Cryptography\n\n- Weak algorithms (MD5, SHA1 for security purposes)\n- Hardcoded IVs or salts\n- Using encryption without authentication (ECB mode, no HMAC)\n- Insufficient key length\n- Using `==` for secret/token comparison instead of `hmac.compare_digest` (leaks timing information)\n\n## Race Conditions\n\nRace conditions are subtle bugs that cause intermittent failures and security vulnerabilities. Pay special attention to:\n\n### Shared State Access\n- Multiple threads accessing shared variables without locks (GIL does not protect multi-step operations)\n- Global state or module-level mutables modified concurrently\n- Lazy initialization without proper locking\n- Non-thread-safe collections used in concurrent context\n\n### Check-Then-Act (TOCTOU)\n- `if (exists) then use` patterns without atomic operations\n- `if (authorized) then perform` where authorization can change\n- File existence check followed by file operation\n- Balance check followed by deduction (financial operations)\n- Inventory check followed by order placement\n- `tempfile.mktemp()` is deprecated (TOCTOU race) \u2014 use `tempfile.mkstemp()` or `NamedTemporaryFile`\n\n### Database Concurrency\n- Missing optimistic locking (`version` column, `updated_at` checks)\n- Missing pessimistic locking (`SELECT FOR UPDATE`)\n- Read-modify-write without transaction isolation\n- Counter increments without atomic operations (`UPDATE SET count = count + 1`)\n- Unique constraint violations in concurrent inserts\n\n### Distributed Systems\n- Missing distributed locks for shared resources\n- Leader election race conditions\n- Cache invalidation races (stale reads after writes)\n- Event ordering dependencies without proper sequencing\n- Split-brain scenarios in cluster operations\n\n### Common Patterns to Flag\n```python\n# Dangerous patterns:\n\n# TOCTOU\nif not os.path.exists(path):\n    open(path, \"w\").write(data)\n\n# Read-modify-write\nvalue = cache.get(key)\nvalue += 1\ncache.set(key, value)\n\n# Check-then-act\nif user.balance >= amount:\n    user.balance -= amount\n    user.save()\n```\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What happens if two requests hit this code simultaneously?\"\n- \"Is this operation atomic or can it be interrupted?\"\n- \"What shared state does this code access?\"\n- \"How does this behave under high concurrency?\"\n\n## Data Integrity\n\n- Missing transactions, partial writes, or inconsistent state updates\n- Weak validation before persistence (implicit type conversion issues)\n- Missing idempotency for retryable operations\n- Lost updates due to concurrent modifications\n\nEND SOURCE\n\nBEGIN SOURCE /home/sergio/.codex/plugins/cache/fr-opus55-581dd0e1-20261011/kk/0.23.0/profiles/python/review-code/solid-checklist.md\nSHA256 5acb4b669369ade34f91f25156866e7f3d0048613dabe362bb8858d90bb254f3\nResolve links relative to this original source path.\n\n# SOLID Smell Prompts\n\n## SRP (Single Responsibility)\n\n- File owns unrelated concerns (e.g., HTTP + DB + domain rules in one file)\n- Large class/module with low cohesion or multiple reasons to change\n- Functions that orchestrate many unrelated steps\n- God objects that know too much about the system\n- Circular imports between modules (signals tangled responsibilities \u2014 causes `ImportError`/`AttributeError` at runtime)\n- **Ask**: \"What is the single reason this module would change?\"\n\n## OCP (Open/Closed)\n\n- Adding a new behavior requires editing many `if/elif` chains (or `match/case` blocks)\n- Feature growth requires modifying core logic rather than extending\n- No plugin/strategy/hook points for variation\n- **Ask**: \"Can I add a new variant without touching existing code?\"\n\n## LSP (Liskov Substitution)\n\n- Subclass checks for concrete type via `isinstance` or raises for base method\n- Overridden methods weaken preconditions or strengthen postconditions\n- Subclass ignores or no-ops parent behavior\n- **Ask**: \"Can I substitute any subclass without the caller knowing?\"\n\n## ISP (Interface Segregation)\n\n- ABCs/Protocols with many methods, most unused by implementers\n- Callers depend on broad interfaces for narrow needs\n- `NotImplementedError` stubs in subclasses\n- **Ask**: \"Do all implementers use all methods?\"\n\n## DIP (Dependency Inversion)\n\n- High-level logic depends on concrete IO, storage, or network types\n- Hard-coded implementations instead of abstractions or injection\n- Import chains that couple business logic to infrastructure\n- **Ask**: \"Can I swap the implementation without changing business logic?\"\n\n---\n\n## Common Code Smells (Beyond SOLID)\n\n| Smell | Signs |\n|-------|-------|\n| **Long method** | Function > 30 lines, multiple levels of nesting |\n| **Feature envy** | Method uses more data from another class than its own |\n| **Data clumps** | Same group of parameters passed together repeatedly |\n| **Primitive obsession** | Using strings/numbers instead of domain types |\n| **Shotgun surgery** | One change requires edits across many files |\n| **Divergent change** | One file changes for many unrelated reasons |\n| **Dead code** | Unreachable or never-called code |\n| **Speculative generality** | Abstractions for hypothetical future needs |\n| **Magic numbers/strings** | Hardcoded values without named constants |\n| **Expensive `@property`** | Property getter performs I/O, DB queries, or heavy computation \u2014 callers expect attribute access to be cheap |\n\n---\n\n## Refactor Heuristics\n\n1. **Split by responsibility, not by size** - A small file can still violate SRP\n2. **Introduce abstraction only when needed** - Wait for the second use case\n3. **Keep refactors incremental** - Isolate behavior before moving\n4. **Preserve behavior first** - Add tests before restructuring\n5. **Name things by intent** - If naming is hard, the abstraction might be wrong\n6. **Prefer composition over inheritance** - Multiple inheritance creates MRO complexity and tight coupling\n7. **Make illegal states unrepresentable** - Use types to enforce invariants\n\nEND SOURCE\n\nBEGIN SOURCE /home/sergio/.codex/plugins/cache/fr-opus55-581dd0e1-20261011/kk/0.23.0/profiles/python/review-code/code-quality-checklist.md\nSHA256 727d2bd6eb1c5fa1216b9b81db3e7d18da08c580dc42dee2d68ae94f08f5748e\nResolve links relative to this original source path.\n\n# Code Quality Checklist\n\n## Contents\n- Error Handling (anti-patterns, best practices, questions)\n- Performance & Caching (CPU, database/IO, caching, memory)\n- Boundary Conditions (None handling, empty collections, numeric, string)\n\n## Error Handling\n\n### Anti-patterns to Flag\n\n- **Swallowed exceptions**: Empty `except` blocks or catch with only logging. Example:\n  ```python\n  try: ...\n  except Exception: pass              # Silent failure\n  try: ...\n  except Exception as e: print(e)     # Log and forget, no re-raise\n  ```\n- **Overly broad except**: Catching bare `Exception` or `BaseException` instead of specific types\n- **Error information leakage**: Stack traces or internal details exposed to users\n- **Missing error handling**: No try-except around fallible operations (I/O, network, parsing)\n- **Async error handling**: Unhandled exceptions in `asyncio` tasks, missing `try/except` in coroutines, fire-and-forget tasks without error callbacks\n\n### Best Practices to Check\n\n- [ ] Errors are caught at appropriate boundaries\n- [ ] Error messages are user-friendly (no internal details exposed)\n- [ ] Errors are logged with sufficient context for debugging\n- [ ] Async task exceptions are retrieved or handled (not silently dropped)\n- [ ] Fallback behavior is defined for recoverable errors\n- [ ] Critical errors trigger alerts/monitoring\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What happens when this operation fails?\"\n- \"Will the caller know something went wrong?\"\n- \"Is there enough context to debug this error?\"\n\n---\n\n## Performance & Caching\n\n### CPU-Intensive Operations\n\n- **Expensive operations in hot paths**: `re.compile` in loops, JSON parsing in hot paths, crypto in loops\n- **Blocking the event loop**: Sync I/O or heavy computation in `async` functions without `run_in_executor`\n- **Unnecessary recomputation**: Same calculation done multiple times\n- **Missing caching**: Pure functions called repeatedly with same inputs, no `@functools.lru_cache` or `@functools.cache`\n- **GIL limitation for CPU-bound work**: `threading` does not parallelize CPU-bound tasks due to the GIL \u2014 use `multiprocessing` or `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor`\n\n### Database & I/O\n\n- **N+1 queries**: Loop that makes a query per item instead of batch\n  ```python\n  # Bad: N+1\n  for id in ids:\n      user = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = %s\", (id,)).fetchone()\n\n  # Good: Batch\n  users = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id IN %s\", (tuple(ids),)).fetchall()\n  ```\n- **Missing indexes**: Queries on unindexed columns\n\nEND PACKET PART 1/2\nInstruction packet checklists: part 2/2\n\n- **Over-fetching**: SELECT * when only few columns needed\n- **No pagination**: Loading entire dataset into memory\n\n### Caching Issues\n\n- **Missing cache for expensive operations**: Repeated API calls, DB queries, computations\n- **Cache without TTL**: Stale data served indefinitely\n- **Cache without invalidation strategy**: Data updated but cache not cleared\n- **Cache key collisions**: Insufficient key uniqueness\n- **Caching user-specific data globally**: Security/privacy issue\n\n### Memory\n\n- **Unbounded collections**: Lists/dicts that grow without limit\n- **Large object retention**: Holding references preventing GC, circular references\n- **String concatenation in loops**: Use `\"\".join(parts)` instead\n- **Loading large files entirely**: Use streaming/iterator patterns instead\n- **Mutable default arguments**: `def foo(items=[])` \u2014 default list is shared across all calls, mutating it causes cross-call contamination\n- **Generator exhaustion**: Iterating a generator/iterator twice silently produces no results on the second pass\n- **Missing context managers**: Not using `with` for files, DB connections, locks \u2014 resources leak on exceptions\n- **Late binding closures**: Lambdas/closures in loops capture the variable by reference, not by value \u2014 all closures see the final loop value\n- **`__del__` unreliability**: `__del__` is not guaranteed to run \u2014 never rely on it for cleanup; use context managers or `atexit`\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What's the time complexity of this operation?\"\n- \"How does this behave with 10x/100x data?\"\n- \"Is this result cacheable? Should it be?\"\n- \"Can this be batched instead of one-by-one?\"\n\n---\n\n## Boundary Conditions\n\n### None Handling\n\n- **Missing None checks**: Accessing attributes on potentially `None` objects (causes `AttributeError`)\n- **Truthiness confusion**: `if value:` when `0`, `\"\"`, `[]`, `{}` are valid values\n- **Excessive None checks**: Deep chains of `if x is not None and x.y is not None` hiding structural issues\n- **`is` vs `==` confusion**: Using `is` to compare values instead of `==` \u2014 works unreliably due to interning (e.g., `x is \"hello\"` may pass for small strings but fail for others)\n- **None vs missing inconsistency**: Mixed usage of `None` and sentinel values without clear convention\n\n### Empty Collections\n\n- **Empty list not handled**: Code assumes list has items\n- **Empty dict edge case**: Iteration or key access on empty dict\n- **First/last element access**: `items[0]` or `items[-1]` without length check\n\n### Numeric Boundaries\n\n- **Division by zero**: Missing check before division (raises `ZeroDivisionError`)\n- **Integer overflow**: Python handles big ints natively, but watch for C-extension or struct boundaries\n- **Floating point comparison**: Using `==` instead of `math.isclose`\n- **Negative values**: Index or count that shouldn't be negative\n- **Off-by-one errors**: Loop bounds, slicing, pagination\n\n### String Boundaries\n\n- **Empty string**: Not handled as edge case\n- **Whitespace-only string**: Passes truthiness check but is effectively empty\n- **Very long strings**: No length limits causing memory/display issues\n- **Unicode edge cases**: Emoji, RTL text, combining characters\n\n### Common Patterns to Flag\n\n```python\n# Dangerous: no None check (AttributeError if user or profile is None)\nname = user.profile.name\n\n# Dangerous: list access without check (IndexError if items is empty)\nfirst = items[0]\n\n# Dangerous: division without check (ZeroDivisionError if count is 0)\navg = total / count\n\n# Dangerous: truthiness check excludes valid values\nif value:  ...   # fails for 0, \"\", [], {}, False\n```\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What if this is None?\"\n- \"What if this collection is empty?\"\n- \"What's the valid range for this number?\"\n- \"What happens at the boundaries (0, -1, sys.maxsize)?\"\n\nEND SOURCE\n\nBEGIN SOURCE     "aggregatedOutput": "# Code Quality Checklist\n\n## Contents\n- Error Handling (anti-patterns, best practices, questions)\n- Performance & Caching (CPU, database/IO, caching, memory)\n- Boundary Conditions (None handling, empty collections, numeric, string)\n\n## Error Handling\n\n### Anti-patterns to Flag\n\n- **Swallowed exceptions**: Empty `except` blocks or catch with only logging. Example:\n  ```python\n  try: ...\n  except Exception: pass              # Silent failure\n  try: ...\n  except Exception as e: print(e)     # Log and forget, no re-raise\n  ```\n- **Overly broad except**: Catching bare `Exception` or `BaseException` instead of specific types\n- **Error information leakage**: Stack traces or internal details exposed to users\n- **Missing error handling**: No try-except around fallible operations (I/O, network, parsing)\n- **Async error handling**: Unhandled exceptions in `asyncio` tasks, missing `try/except` in coroutines, fire-and-forget tasks without error callbacks\n\n### Best Practices to Check\n\n- [ ] Errors are caught at appropriate boundaries\n- [ ] Error messages are user-friendly (no internal details exposed)\n- [ ] Errors are logged with sufficient context for debugging\n- [ ] Async task exceptions are retrieved or handled (not silently dropped)\n- [ ] Fallback behavior is defined for recoverable errors\n- [ ] Critical errors trigger alerts/monitoring\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What happens when this operation fails?\"\n- \"Will the caller know something went wrong?\"\n- \"Is there enough context to debug this error?\"\n\n---\n\n## Performance & Caching\n\n### CPU-Intensive Operations\n\n- **Expensive operations in hot paths**: `re.compile` in loops, JSON parsing in hot paths, crypto in loops\n- **Blocking the event loop**: Sync I/O or heavy computation in `async` functions without `run_in_executor`\n- **Unnecessary recomputation**: Same calculation done multiple times\n- **Missing caching**: Pure functions called repeatedly with same inputs, no `@functools.lru_cache` or `@functools.cache`\n- **GIL limitation for CPU-bound work**: `threading` does not parallelize CPU-bound tasks due to the GIL \u2014 use `multiprocessing` or `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor`\n\n### Database & I/O\n\n- **N+1 queries**: Loop that makes a query per item instead of batch\n  ```python\n  # Bad: N+1\n  for id in ids:\n      user = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = %s\", (id,)).fetchone()\n\n  # Good: Batch\n  users = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id IN %s\", (tuple(ids),)).fetchall()\n  ```\n- **Missing indexes**: Queries on unindexed columns\n- **Over-fetching**: SELECT * when only few columns needed\n- **No pagination**: Loading entire dataset into memory\n\n### Caching Issues\n\n- **Missing cache for expensive operations**: Repeated API calls, DB queries, computations\n- **Cache without TTL**: Stale data served indefinitely\n- **Cache without invalidation strategy**: Data updated but cache not cleared\n- **Cache key collisions**: Insufficient key uniqueness\n- **Caching user-specific data globally**: Security/privacy issue\n\n### Memory\n\n- **Unbounded collections**: Lists/dicts that grow without limit\n- **Large object retention**: Holding references preventing GC, circular references\n- **String concatenation in loops**: Use `\"\".join(parts)` instead\n- **Loading large files entirely**: Use streaming/iterator patterns instead\n- **Mutable default arguments**: `def foo(items=[])` \u2014 default list is shared across all calls, mutating it causes cross-call contamination\n- **Generator exhaustion**: Iterating a generator/iterator twice silently produces no results on the second pass\n- **Missing context managers**: Not using `with` for files, DB connections, locks \u2014 resources leak on exceptions\n- **Late binding closures**: Lambdas/closures in loops capture the variable by reference, not by value \u2014 all closures see the final loop value\n- **`__del__` unreliability**: `__del__` is not guaranteed to run \u2014 never rely on it for cleanup; use context managers or `atexit`\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What's the time complexity of this operation?\"\n- \"How does this behave with 10x/100x data?\"\n- \"Is this result cacheable? Should it be?\"\n- \"Can this be batched instead of one-by-one?\"\n\n---\n\n## Boundary Conditions\n\n### None Handling\n\n- **Missing None checks**: Accessing attributes on potentially `None` objects (causes `AttributeError`)\n- **Truthiness confusion**: `if value:` when `0`, `\"\"`, `[]`, `{}` are valid values\n- **Excessive None checks**: Deep chains of `if x is not None and x.y is not None` hiding structural issues\n- **`is` vs `==` confusion**: Using `is` to compare values instead of `==` \u2014 works unreliably due to interning (e.g., `x is \"hello\"` may pass for small strings but fail for others)\n- **None vs missing inconsistency**: Mixed usage of `None` and sentinel values without clear convention\n\n### Empty Collections\n\n- **Empty list not handled**: Code assumes list has items\n- **Empty dict edge case**: Iteration or key access on empty dict\n- **First/last element access**: `items[0]` or `items[-1]` without length check\n\n### Numeric Boundaries\n\n- **Division by zero**: Missing check before division (raises `ZeroDivisionError`)\n- **Integer overflow**: Python handles big ints natively, but watch for C-extension or struct boundaries\n- **Floating point comparison**: Using `==` instead of `math.isclose`\n- **Negative values**: Index or count that shouldn't be negative\n- **Off-by-one errors**: Loop bounds, slicing, pagination\n\n### String Boundaries\n\n- **Empty string**: Not handled as edge case\n- **Whitespace-only string**: Passes truthiness check but is effectively empty\n- **Very long strings**: No length limits causing memory/display issues\n- **Unicode edge cases**: Emoji, RTL text, combining characters\n\n### Common Patterns to Flag\n\n```python\n# Dangerous: no None check (AttributeError if user or profile is None)\nname = user.profile.name\n\n# Dangerous: list access without check (IndexError if items is empty)\nfirst = items[0]\n\n# Dangerous: division without check (ZeroDivisionError if count is 0)\navg = total / count\n\n# Dangerous: truthiness check excludes valid values\nif value:  ...   # fails for 0, \"\", [], {}, False\n```\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What if this is None?\"\n- \"What if this collection is empty?\"\n- \"What's the valid range for this number?\"\n- \"What happens at the boundaries (0, -1, sys.maxsize)?\"\n",

and security vulnerabilities. Pay special attention to:\n\n### Shared State Access\n- Multiple threads accessing shared variables without locks (GIL does not protect multi-step operations)\n- Global state or module-level mutables modified concurrently\n- Lazy initialization without proper locking\n- Non-thread-safe collections used in concurrent context\n\n### Check-Then-Act (TOCTOU)\n- `if (exists) then use` patterns without atomic operations\n- `if (authorized) then perform` where authorization can change\n- File existence check followed by file operation\n- Balance check followed by deduction (financial operations)\n- Inventory check followed by order placement\n- `tempfile.mktemp()` is deprecated (TOCTOU race) \u2014 use `tempfile.mkstemp()` or `NamedTemporaryFile`\n\n### Database Concurrency\n- Missing optimistic value)\n\n# Check-then-act\nif user.balance >= amount:\n    user.balance -= amount\n    user.save()\n```\n\n### Questions to Ask\n- \"What happens if two requests hit this code simultaneously?\"\n- \"Is this operation atomic or can it be interrupted?\"\n- \"What shared state does this code access?\"\n- \"How does this behave under high concurrency?\"\n\n## Data Integrity\n\n- Missing transactions, partial writes, or inconsistent state updates\n- Weak validation before persistence (implicit type conversion issues)\n- Missing idempotency for retryable operations\n- Lost updates due to concurrent modifications\n",

Scale it to the change: a few sentences for a helper fix, a compact scenario table for a cross-component mutation. Omit empty boilerplate; explain briefly why an operational dimension is irrelevant.\n\n## Minimum useful record\n\n| Field | Content |\n| --- | --- |\n| Intent and authority | Intended outcomes and constraints; source locations; explicit, inferred or unresolved status. |\n| Change boundary | Current task/request, resolved diff scope, candidate revision/worktree state, affected components and deliberately pending functionality. |\n| Existing behavior | Entry points, unchanged callers/consumers, state/data contracts and invariants to preserve. |\n| Delivery constraints | Applicable repository policy, independent/ordered deployment expectations, flags, migrations, prerequisites and accepted exceptions. |\n| Baselines | Review base and candidate; separately, relevant released/deployed revisions, configurations and data shapes; explicit unknowns. |\n| Scenarios and evidence | Success, boundaries, failure/retry and lifecycle cases that apply; source/test bindings, observed results and unverified conditions. |\n\nUser decisions and applicable repository instructions establish requirements. Consult current design/task contracts and relevant linked issues without assuming an issue is newer or more authoritative. Explain consequential conflicts. Existing code establishes behavior, not product intent; tests can repeat an implementation's mistaken assumption. Without a specification, infer a provisional purpose from the request and inspected evidence and label it. Ask only when materially different interpretations prevent a useful judgment; continue independent investigation.\n\nNever equate the review base, local main, a stable tag and a deployed revision. Name the baseline actually inspected. Source compatibility with a tag does not verify a deployed environment. Missing documents or production access narrow conclusions; they neither disable functional reasoning nor create automatic access or approval requirements.\n\n## Producer and reviewer responsibilities\n\nThe producer supplies facts with file/symbol or revision references, separating assumptions, accepted decisions and unavailable evidence. Author explanations and claimed test results remain attributed inputs, not independent verification.     "aggregatedOutput": "# SOLID Smell Prompts\n\n## SRP (Single Responsibility)\n\n- File owns unrelated concerns (e.g., HTTP + DB + domain rules in one file)\n- Large class/module with low cohesion or multiple reasons to change\n- Functions that orchestrate many unrelated steps\n- God objects that know too much about the system\n- Circular imports between modules (signals tangled responsibilities \u2014 causes `ImportError`/`AttributeError` at runtime)\n- **Ask**: \"What is the single reason this module would change?\"\n\n## OCP (Open/Closed)\n\n- Adding a new behavior requires editing many `if/elif` chains (or `match/case` blocks)\n- Feature growth requires modifying core logic rather than extending\n- No plugin/strategy/hook points for variation\n- **Ask**: \"Can I add a new variant without touching existing code?\"\n\n## LSP (Liskov Substitution)\n\n- Subclass checks for concrete type via `isinstance` or raises for base method\n- Overridden methods weaken preconditions or strengthen postconditions\n- Subclass ignores or no-ops parent behavior\n- **Ask**: \"Can I substitute any subclass without the caller knowing?\"\n\n## ISP (Interface Segregation)\n\n- ABCs/Protocols with many methods, most unused by implementers\n- Callers depend on broad interfaces for narrow needs\n- `NotImplementedError` stubs in subclasses\n- **Ask**: \"Do all implementers use all methods?\"\n\n## DIP (Dependency Inversion)\n\n- High-level logic depends on concrete IO, storage, or network types\n- Hard-coded implementations instead of abstractions or injection\n- Import chains that couple business logic to infrastructure\n- **Ask**: \"Can I swap the implementation without changing business logic?\"\n\n---\n\n## Common Code Smells (Beyond SOLID)\n\n| Smell | Signs |\n|-------|-------|\n| **Long method** | Function > 30 lines, multiple levels of nesting |\n| **Feature envy** | Method uses more data from another class than its own |\n| **Data clumps** | Same group of parameters passed together repeatedly |\n| **Primitive obsession** | Using strings/numbers instead of domain types |\n| **Shotgun surgery** | One change requires edits across many files |\n| **Divergent change** | One file changes for many unrelated reasons |\n| **Dead code** | Unreachable or never-called code |\n| **Speculative generality** | Abstractions for hypothetical future needs |\n| **Magic numbers/strings** | Hardcoded values without named constants |\n| **Expensive `@property`** | Property getter performs I/O, DB queries, or heavy computation \u2014 callers expect attribute access to be cheap |\n\n---\n\n## Refactor Heuristics\n\n1. **Split by responsibility, not by size** - A small file can still violate SRP\n2. **Introduce abstraction only when needed** - Wait for the second use case\n3. **Keep refactors incremental** - Isolate behavior before moving\n4. **Preserve behavior first** - Add tests before restructuring\n5. **Name things by intent** - If naming is hard, the abstraction might be wrong\n6. **Prefer composition over inheritance** - Multiple inheritance creates MRO complexity and tight coupling\n7. **Make illegal states unrepresentable** - Use types to enforce invariants\n",

Without scope, they flag code-that-isn't-written-yet as `MISSING_IMPL` (spec review) or as missing functionality (code review). Standard review uses this scope locally; isolated review injects the same artifact into sub-agent and external-reviewer prompts. The existing $kk:review-spec payload remains sufficient; no additional change-context record is required by this protocol.\n\n**Code-review timing:** apply this protocol's task-document reads during the\npost-instruction investigation phase, after every resolved checklist is loaded.\nEarly diff selection uses filenames and Git metadata; it is distinct from\ndetermining task scope. Reading this protocol does not authorize early inspection\nof a discovered task file's requirements. The current task id is known (the one just finished). Use full scope filtering.\n2. **Invoked directly inside a feature** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists but no current task is explicitly supplied. Derive scope from `tasks.md` status fields (`done` = in scope, `pending`/`in-progress` = out of scope).\n3. **Invoked on an arbitrary diff** \u2014 no `/docs/feat/wip/` directory relates to the diff. Skip the scope artifact entirely and note \"No task scope available\" in the reviewer prompt.\n\n### Scope artifact format\n\nWhen shape 1 or 2 applies, build this block and inject it into every reviewer prompt (sub-agent prompt template and the external-reviewer `step` framing):\n\n```\n## Task Scope\n\nReview mode: {mid-implementation | post-implementation}\n\nIn scope (review these):\n- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: done\n- [...more done tasks if applicable]\n\nOut of scope (pending \u2014 DO NOT flag as missing):\n- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: {pending|in-progress}\n- [...more pending tasks]\n\nThe feature's design docs describe the full end state. Pending tasks are expected gaps in the current diff \u2014 treat their absence from the code as intentional, not as a finding. You may still flag issues within the in-scope tasks even if they reference pending tasks (e.g., a broken interface contract). Pending work does not excuse current regressions or incompatibility: assess whether the reviewed increment preserves existing flows and meets applicable delivery requirements. Do not invent a current regression or require pending work when the increment remains compatible under its actual delivery contract.\n- If a finding would be valid *only* after pending work lands, mention it under \"Areas Not Covered\" instead of the P0\u2013P3 sections.\n",
